On April 29, 2017, Spears became the first recipient of the Icon Award at the [2017 Radio Disney Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Radio_Disney_Music_Awards "2017 Radio Disney Music Awards").[&#8202;](#ref-back-226-0)[\[226\]](#refnote-226) On November 4, 2017, Spears attended the grand opening of the Nevada Childhood Cancer Foundation Britney Spears Campus in [Las Vegas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Vegas "Las Vegas").[&#8202;](#ref-back-227-0)[\[227\]](#refnote-227) Later that month, _[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes "Forbes")_ announced that Spears was the eighth-highest-earning female musician, earning $34 million in 2017.[&#8202;](#ref-back-228-0)[\[228\]](#refnote-228) On December 31, 2017, Spears performed the final show of Britney: Piece of Me.[&#8202;](#ref-back-iheart.com-0)[\[229\]](#refnote-iheart.com) The final performance reportedly brought in $1.172 million, setting a new box office record for a single show in Las Vegas, a record previously held by [Jennifer Lopez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Lopez "Jennifer Lopez").[&#8202;](#ref-back-iheart.com-1)[\[229\]](#refnote-iheart.com) Performances of " [Toxic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toxic_(song) "Toxic (song)")" and " [Work Bitch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Work_Bitch "Work Bitch")" were recorded on earlier dates and aired on ABC's _[Dick Clark's New Year's Rockin' Eve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Clark's_New_Year's_Rockin'_Eve "Dick Clark's New Year's Rockin' Eve")_ to a record audience of 25.6 million.[&#8202;](#ref-back-230-0)[\[230\]](#refnote-230)

In January 2018, Spears released her 24th perfume with Elizabeth Arden, [Sunset Fantasy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fantasy_(fragrance)#Sunset_Fantasy "Fantasy (fragrance)"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-231-0)[\[231\]](#refnote-231) and announced the [Piece of Me Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piece_of_Me_Tour "Piece of Me Tour"), which took place in July 2018 in North America and Europe.[&#8202;](#ref-back-232-0)[\[232\]](#refnote-232) Tickets were sold out within minutes for major cities, and additional dates were added to meet the demand.[&#8202;](#ref-back-233-0)[\[233\]](#refnote-233) [Pitbull](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pitbull_(rapper) "Pitbull (rapper)") was the [supporting act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supporting_act "Supporting act") for the European leg.[&#8202;](#ref-back-234-0)[\[234\]](#refnote-234) The tour ranked at 86 in North America and 30 worldwide on _[Pollstar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pollstar "Pollstar")_'s 2018 Year-End Top 100 Tours chart. In total, the tour grossed $54.3 million, with 260,531 tickets sold, and was the sixth-highest-grossing female tour of 2018. It was also the United Kingdom's second-best-selling female tour of 2018.[&#8202;](#ref-back-235-0)[\[235\]](#refnote-235)[&#8202;](#ref-back-236-0)[\[236\]](#refnote-236)

On March 20, 2018, Spears was announced as part of a campaign for French luxury fashion house [Kenzo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenzo_(brand) "Kenzo (brand)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-harpersbazaar.com.au-0)[\[237\]](#refnote-harpersbazaar.com.au) The company said it aimed to shake up the "jungle" world of fashion with Spears's "La Collection Memento No. 2" campaign.[&#8202;](#ref-back-lvmh.com-0)[\[238\]](#refnote-lvmh.com) On April 12, 2018, Spears was honored with the 2018 [GLAAD Vanguard Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GLAAD_Vanguard_Award "GLAAD Vanguard Award") at the [GLAAD Media Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GLAAD_Media_Award "GLAAD Media Award") for her role in "accelerating acceptance for the LGBTQ community".[&#8202;](#ref-back-spearsglaad-0)[\[239\]](#refnote-spearsglaad) On April 27, 2018, Epic Rights announced a new partnership with Spears to debut her own fashion line in 2019, which would include clothing, fitness apparel, accessories, and electronics.[&#8202;](#ref-back-240-0)[\[240\]](#refnote-240)

In July 2018, Spears released her first unisex fragrance, [Prerogative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britney_Spears_products#Fragrances "Britney Spears products").[&#8202;](#ref-back-241-0)[\[241\]](#refnote-241) On October 18, 2018, Spears announced her second Las Vegas residency show, Britney: Domination, which was set to launch at [Park MGM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Park_MGM "Park MGM")'s Park Theatre on February 13, 2019.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:0-0)[\[242\]](#refnote-:0) Spears was slated to make $507,000 per show, which would have made her the highest paid act on the [Las Vegas Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Vegas_Strip "Las Vegas Strip").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:0-1)[\[242\]](#refnote-:0)[&#8202;](#ref-back-243-0)[\[243\]](#refnote-243) On October 21, 2018, Spears performed at the [Formula One Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Grand_Prix "United States Grand Prix") in [Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austin,_Texas "Austin, Texas"), the final performance of her Piece of Me Tour.[&#8202;](#ref-back-244-0)[\[244\]](#refnote-244)

### 2019–2021: Conservatorship dispute, \#FreeBritney, and abuse allegations

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Free_Britney_Movement_in_Washington_1.jpg/250px-Free_Britney_Movement_in_Washington_1.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Free_Britney_Movement_in_Washington_1.jpg) The #FreeBritney movement in front of the [Lincoln Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Memorial "Lincoln Memorial"), 2021

Main article: [Britney Spears conservatorship case](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britney_Spears_conservatorship_case "Britney Spears conservatorship case")

By early 2019, Spears announced an indefinite hiatus and the cancellation of her Las Vegas residency after her father, Jamie, suffered a near-fatal colon rupture and Andrew Wallet resigned as co-conservator of her estate.[&#8202;](#ref-back-245-0)[\[245\]](#refnote-245)[&#8202;](#ref-back-246-0)[\[246\]](#refnote-246) Following allegations of Jamie's involvement in cancelling Spears's residency and placing her in a psychiatric facility,[&#8202;](#ref-back-247-0)[\[247\]](#refnote-247)[&#8202;](#ref-back-248-0)[\[248\]](#refnote-248) a movement titled #FreeBritney was organized in May 2019.[&#8202;](#ref-back-whatisactually-0)[\[249\]](#refnote-whatisactually) Judge Brenda Penny announced a professional evaluation of the conservatorship that month.[&#8202;](#ref-back-250-0)[\[250\]](#refnote-250) That September, Kevin Federline filed a restraining order against Jamie following an alleged physical altercation between Jamie and one of her sons.[&#8202;](#ref-back-251-0)[\[251\]](#refnote-251)

An interactive pop-up museum dedicated to Spears, dubbed "The Zone", opened in Los Angeles in February 2020, though it was later suspended due to the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic "COVID-19 pandemic").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Shapiro_2020-0)[\[252\]](#refnote-Shapiro_2020)[&#8202;](#ref-back-253-0)[\[253\]](#refnote-253) She released _Glory_'s Japanese-exclusive bonus track, " [Mood Ring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mood_Ring_(Britney_Spears_song) "Mood Ring (Britney Spears song)")" as a single, and debuted a new cover of the album to streaming and digital platforms worldwide in May 2020.[&#8202;](#ref-back-254-0)[\[254\]](#refnote-254) In December, Spears released an updated deluxe edition of _Glory_, which includes "Mood Ring" and new songs " [Swimming in the Stars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swimming_in_the_Stars "Swimming in the Stars")" and " [Matches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matches_(Britney_Spears_and_Backstreet_Boys_song) "Matches (Britney Spears and Backstreet Boys song)")", a collaboration with [Backstreet Boys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Backstreet_Boys "Backstreet Boys").[&#8202;](#ref-back-255-0)[\[255\]](#refnote-255)

In February 2021, a television documentary titled _[Framing Britney Spears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Framing_Britney_Spears "Framing Britney Spears")_ aired on FX.[&#8202;](#ref-back-256-0)[\[256\]](#refnote-256) That June, _[The New York Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_York_Times "The New York Times")_ obtained confidential court documents stating that Spears had pushed for years to end her conservatorship.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:3-1)[\[3\]](#refnote-:3) At the conservatorship hearing, Spears announced her intentions to terminate the conservatorship, citing abuse.[&#8202;](#ref-back-courtaddress-0)[\[257\]](#refnote-courtaddress)[&#8202;](#ref-back-258-0)[\[258\]](#refnote-258)[&#8202;](#ref-back-259-0)[\[259\]](#refnote-259) In July, Bessemer Trust and Spears's longtime manager [Larry Rudolph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larry_Rudolph "Larry Rudolph") resigned.[&#8202;](#ref-back-260-0)[\[260\]](#refnote-260)[&#8202;](#ref-back-261-0)[\[261\]](#refnote-261) Later in the year, Spears announced her engagement to her longtime boyfriend, Sam Asghari.[&#8202;](#ref-back-262-0)[\[262\]](#refnote-262) After Jamie filed a suit to end his involvement, Penny terminated Spears's conservatorship in November.[&#8202;](#ref-back-263-0)[\[263\]](#refnote-263)[&#8202;](#ref-back-264-0)[\[264\]](#refnote-264)[&#8202;](#ref-back-NYTNov21-0)[\[265\]](#refnote-NYTNov21)

### 2022–present: Third marriage, _The Woman in Me_, and retirement from the music industry

In April 2022, she announced her pregnancy with Asghari's child,[&#8202;](#ref-back-266-0)[\[266\]](#refnote-266) which ended in a miscarriage the following month.[&#8202;](#ref-back-267-0)[\[267\]](#refnote-267) The couple married on June 9 at her home in [Thousand Oaks, Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thousand_Oaks,_California "Thousand Oaks, California").[&#8202;](#ref-back-268-0)[\[268\]](#refnote-268) None of Spears's immediate family (including her parents, sister, and brother) were invited; her two sons did not attend.[&#8202;](#ref-back-family_not_invited-0)[\[269\]](#refnote-family_not_invited)[&#8202;](#ref-back-sons_not_at_wedding-0)[\[270\]](#refnote-sons_not_at_wedding) Spears's ex-husband, Jason Alexander, attempted to crash the wedding by breaking into her home, armed with a knife, but was arrested.[&#8202;](#ref-back-271-0)[\[271\]](#refnote-271) A three-year [restraining order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Restraining_order "Restraining order") was subsequently enacted.[&#8202;](#ref-back-272-0)[\[272\]](#refnote-272) On August 26, Spears and English musician [Elton John](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elton_John "Elton John") released the duet " [Hold Me Closer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hold_Me_Closer_(Elton_John_and_Britney_Spears_song) "Hold Me Closer (Elton John and Britney Spears song)")", a remake of John's 1972 single " [Tiny Dancer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiny_Dancer "Tiny Dancer")".[&#8202;](#ref-back-273-0)[\[273\]](#refnote-273)[&#8202;](#ref-back-274-0)[\[274\]](#refnote-274) It was Spears's first musical release since dissolving her conservatorship.[&#8202;](#ref-back-275-0)[\[275\]](#refnote-275) "Hold Me Closer" debuted at number six on the US [_Billboard_ Hot 100](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard_Hot_100 "Billboard Hot 100"), becoming her 14th top-ten single and her highest-charting song in the chart since " [Scream & Shout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scream_&_Shout "Scream & Shout")" (2012).[&#8202;](#ref-back-276-0)[\[276\]](#refnote-276) It debuted at number three on the [UK Singles Chart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK_Singles_Chart "UK Singles Chart"), earning Spears her 24th top-ten.[&#8202;](#ref-back-277-0)[\[277\]](#refnote-277)

Since her conservatorship's dissolution, Spears's personal life, social media presence, and overall well-being have been subject to renewed media interest and fan speculation,[&#8202;](#ref-back-278-0)[\[278\]](#refnote-278) giving rise to conspiracy theories.[&#8202;](#ref-back-279-0)[\[279\]](#refnote-279) On January 24, 2023, deputies from the [Ventura County Sheriff's Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ventura_County_Sheriff's_Office "Ventura County Sheriff's Office") performed a welfare check at Spears's residence after receiving several calls from fans who were concerned after she deleted her [Instagram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instagram "Instagram") account. A spokesperson for the Sheriff's Department stated that Spears "was safe and in no danger". Spears addressed the incident on her [Twitter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter "Twitter") account, asking fans to respect her privacy.[&#8202;](#ref-back-280-0)[\[280\]](#refnote-280) Spears and the rapper [will.i.am](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Will.i.am "Will.i.am") released their single, " [Mind Your Business](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mind_Your_Business_(song) "Mind Your Business (song)")", on July 21, 2023.[&#8202;](#ref-back-281-0)[\[281\]](#refnote-281) On August 16, it was announced Spears and Asghari separated after 14 months of marriage.[&#8202;](#ref-back-282-0)[\[282\]](#refnote-282) On May 1, 2024, they reached a divorce settlement.[&#8202;](#ref-back-283-0)[\[283\]](#refnote-283)[&#8202;](#ref-back-284-0)[\[284\]](#refnote-284) The following day, the judge signed off on the settlement and the couple were divorced on December 2, 2024.[&#8202;](#ref-back-285-0)[\[285\]](#refnote-285) In September 2023, an additional welfare check was initiated when Spears posted an Instagram video of herself dancing with knives. Her security team assured the attending officer that there was no immediate threat to her safety, and the officer departed. Spears also clarified that the knives were not real.[&#8202;](#ref-back-286-0)[\[286\]](#refnote-286)

After signing a $15 million book deal in February 2022 in one of the [largest deals ever made](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_book_deals "List of largest book deals"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-287-0)[\[287\]](#refnote-287)[&#8202;](#ref-back-biggest_book_deal-0)[\[288\]](#refnote-biggest_book_deal) she published a second memoir, _[The Woman in Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Woman_in_Me_(memoir) "The Woman in Me (memoir)"),_ in October 2023.[&#8202;](#ref-back-289-0)[\[289\]](#refnote-289) It details her rise to fame, public media events, her conservatorship, and her newfound freedom.[&#8202;](#ref-back-290-0)[\[290\]](#refnote-290) In the United States, it sold 1.1 million copies, while worldwide it sold 2.4 million copies in print sales during its first week of release.[&#8202;](#ref-back-291-0)[\[291\]](#refnote-291)[&#8202;](#ref-back-292-0)[\[292\]](#refnote-292)

In January 2024, reports circulated that [Charli XCX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charli_XCX "Charli XCX") and [Julia Michaels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julia_Michaels "Julia Michaels") had been asked to write songs for Spears, and _[Rolling Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone "Rolling Stone")_ reported that "management and A&R are trying to get her excited for the music". Spears denied the reports, saying she would never return to the music industry.[&#8202;](#ref-back-CatalogueBBC-0)[\[293\]](#refnote-CatalogueBBC) However, she also said that she had written more than 20 songs for other artists in the previous two years.[&#8202;](#ref-back-294-0)[\[294\]](#refnote-294) In May 2024, Charli XCX confirmed in an interview that she had indeed been asked to write for Spears, for which she traveled to Malibu, but that she did not know if Spears was involved, explaining that "the team were present... But she didn't record it. She obviously didn't."[&#8202;](#ref-back-295-0)[\[295\]](#refnote-295)

In 2026, she clarified her exit from the music industry, saying that she would "never perform in the US again", but did not rule out performing elsewhere, saying "I hope to be ... performing with my son … in the UK and Australia very soon."[&#8202;](#ref-back-:4-0)[\[296\]](#refnote-:4)[&#8202;](#ref-back-297-0)[\[297\]](#refnote-297)

In February 2026, _[Variety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variety_(magazine) "Variety (magazine)")_ reported that music publisher [Primary Wave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_Wave_(company) "Primary Wave (company)") had acquired the rights to Spears's entire music catalog along with other rights.[&#8202;](#ref-back-298-0)[\[298\]](#refnote-298) The [BBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC "BBC") reported that the purchase took place on December 30, 2025, for roughly $200 million.[&#8202;](#ref-back-CatalogueBBC-1)[\[293\]](#refnote-CatalogueBBC) The following month, she was arrested in [Ventura County, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ventura_County,_California "Ventura County, California"), on suspicion of [driving under the influence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Driving_under_the_influence "Driving under the influence").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:4-1)[\[296\]](#refnote-:4)[&#8202;](#ref-back-299-0)[\[299\]](#refnote-299) In April, Spears's representative stated that she had checked herself into a rehabilitation facility.[&#8202;](#ref-back-SpearsRehab2026-0)[\[300\]](#refnote-SpearsRehab2026) On April 30, 2026, she officially received a criminal DUI charge.[&#8202;](#ref-back-chargeddui-0)[\[301\]](#refnote-chargeddui)[&#8202;](#ref-back-302-0)[\[302\]](#refnote-302) Spears was scheduled to appear in court for her arraignment hearing May 4, 2026.[&#8202;](#ref-back-chargeddui-1)[\[301\]](#refnote-chargeddui)[&#8202;](#ref-back-arraignmentscheduledandoffer-0)[\[303\]](#refnote-arraignmentscheduledandoffer) It had been agreed that Spears could avoid jail time for this charge if she pled guilty to [reckless driving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reckless_driving "Reckless driving"), for which she would only get [probation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Probation "Probation") and be required to both complete a DUI class and pay state-mandated fines and fees.[&#8202;](#ref-back-arraignmentscheduledandoffer-1)[\[303\]](#refnote-arraignmentscheduledandoffer)

On April 30, 2026, Spears checked out of rehab.[&#8202;](#ref-back-304-0)[\[304\]](#refnote-304) On May 4, 2026, Spears, who was absent from court during her arraignment hearing and had her defense attorney appear on her behalf, pled guilty in a Ventura County court to one count of reckless driving.[&#8202;](#ref-back-guiltypleamay2026-0)[\[305\]](#refnote-guiltypleamay2026) She was then sentenced to one year of probation and one day in jail, with credit for the time in jail she served following her March 2026 arrest.[&#8202;](#ref-back-guiltypleamay2026-1)[\[305\]](#refnote-guiltypleamay2026)[&#8202;](#ref-back-spearsmay2026sentence-0)[\[306\]](#refnote-spearsmay2026sentence) In addition, Spears was ordered to meet with a [psychologist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychologist "Psychologist") once a week and a [psychiatrist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychiatrist "Psychiatrist") twice a month.[&#8202;](#ref-back-spearsmay2026sentence-1)[\[306\]](#refnote-spearsmay2026sentence)[&#8202;](#ref-back-guiltypleamay2026-2)[\[305\]](#refnote-guiltypleamay2026) She was also fined $571 and is required to attend a three-month DUI program which sees drivers complete 30 hours of class time. As part of her plea deal, Spears' vehicle became authorized to be searched for drugs and alcohol.[&#8202;](#ref-back-spearsmay2026sentence-2)[\[306\]](#refnote-spearsmay2026sentence)

## Artistry

### Influences

[![Madonna performing](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/Madonna_Rebel_Heart_Tour_2015_-_Stockholm_%2823051472299%29_%28cropped_2%29.jpg/250px-Madonna_Rebel_Heart_Tour_2015_-_Stockholm_%2823051472299%29_%28cropped_2%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Madonna_Rebel_Heart_Tour_2015_-_Stockholm_(23051472299)_(cropped_2).jpg)

[![Janet Jackson performing](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/JanetJacksonUnbreakableTourSanFran2015_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-JanetJacksonUnbreakableTourSanFran2015_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:JanetJacksonUnbreakableTourSanFran2015_(cropped).jpg)

[![Whitney Houston performing](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/Whitney_Houston_%28cropped3%29.JPEG/250px-Whitney_Houston_%28cropped3%29.JPEG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Whitney_Houston_(cropped3).JPEG)

Spears credits artists such as (left to right) [Madonna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna "Madonna"), [Janet Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janet_Jackson "Janet Jackson"), and [Whitney Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitney_Houston "Whitney Houston") as major musical influences.

Spears has cited [Madonna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna "Madonna"), [Janet Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janet_Jackson "Janet Jackson"), and [Whitney Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitney_Houston "Whitney Houston") as major influences, her "three favorite artists" as a child, whom she would "sing along to... day and night in \[her\] living room"; Houston's " [I Have Nothing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Have_Nothing "I Have Nothing")" was the song she auditioned to that landed her record deal with Jive Records.[&#8202;](#ref-back-307-0)[\[307\]](#refnote-307) Spears also named [Mariah Carey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariah_Carey "Mariah Carey") as "one of the main reasons I started singing".[&#8202;](#ref-back-308-0)[\[308\]](#refnote-308) Throughout her career, Spears has drawn frequent comparisons to Madonna and Jackson in particular, in terms of vocals, choreography, and stage presence. According to Spears: "I know when I was younger, I looked up to people... like, you know, Janet Jackson and Madonna. And they were major inspirations for me. But I also had my own identity and I knew who I was."[&#8202;](#ref-back-309-0)[\[309\]](#refnote-309)

In the 2002 book _Madonnastyle_ by Carol Clerk, Spears is quoted saying: "I have been a huge fan of Madonna since I was a little girl. She's the person that I've really looked up to. I would really, really like to be a legend like Madonna."[&#8202;](#ref-back-310-0)[\[310\]](#refnote-310) Spears cited " [That's the Way Love Goes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/That's_the_Way_Love_Goes_(Janet_Jackson_song) "That's the Way Love Goes (Janet Jackson song)")" as the inspiration for her song "Touch of My Hand" from her album _In the Zone_, saying "I like to compare it to 'That's the Way Love Goes,' kind of a Janet Jackson thing."[&#8202;](#ref-back-311-0)[\[311\]](#refnote-311) She also said her song "Just Luv Me" from her _Glory_ album also reminded her of "That's the Way Love Goes".[&#8202;](#ref-back-312-0)[\[312\]](#refnote-312)

After personally meeting Spears, Jackson stated: "she said to me, 'I'm such a big fan; I really admire you.' That's so flattering. Everyone gets inspiration from some place. And it's awesome to see someone else coming up who's dancing and singing, and seeing how all these kids relate to her. A lot of people put it down, but what she does is a positive thing."[&#8202;](#ref-back-313-0)[\[313\]](#refnote-313) Madonna said of Spears in the documentary _[Britney: For the Record](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britney:_For_the_Record "Britney: For the Record")_: "I admire her talent as an artist... There are aspects about her that I recognize in myself when I first started out in my career."[&#8202;](#ref-back-314-0)[\[314\]](#refnote-314) Spears has also named [Michael Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson "Michael Jackson"), [Mariah Carey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariah_Carey "Mariah Carey"), [Sheryl Crow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheryl_Crow "Sheryl Crow"), [Otis Redding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otis_Redding "Otis Redding"), [Shania Twain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shania_Twain "Shania Twain"), [Brandy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandy_Norwood "Brandy Norwood"), [Beyoncé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beyonc%C3%A9 "Beyoncé"), [Natalie Imbruglia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natalie_Imbruglia "Natalie Imbruglia"), [Cher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cher "Cher"), and [Prince](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_(musician) "Prince (musician)") as inspirations,[&#8202;](#ref-back-shane-0)[\[315\]](#refnote-shane)[&#8202;](#ref-back-316-0)[\[316\]](#refnote-316)[&#8202;](#ref-back-317-0)[\[317\]](#refnote-317) and younger artists such as [Selena Gomez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selena_Gomez "Selena Gomez") and [Ariana Grande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariana_Grande "Ariana Grande").[&#8202;](#ref-back-318-0)[\[318\]](#refnote-318)[&#8202;](#ref-back-319-0)[\[319\]](#refnote-319)

### Musical style

Spears is described as a [pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_music "Pop music") artist[&#8202;](#ref-back-bsbiography-1)[\[1\]](#refnote-bsbiography) and generally explores the genre in the form of [dance-pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dance-pop "Dance-pop").[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTEShuker201299-0)[\[320\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTEShuker201299)[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTEAppellHemphill2006"Dance_Pop"-0)[\[321\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTEAppellHemphill2006"Dance_Pop")[&#8202;](#ref-back-wtimes2012-0)[\[322\]](#refnote-wtimes2012) Following her debut, she was credited with influencing the revival of [teen pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teen_pop "Teen pop") in the late 1990s.[&#8202;](#ref-back-rsspears-0)[\[323\]](#refnote-rsspears) Rob Sheffield of _Rolling Stone_ wrote: "Spears carries on the classic archetype of the rock & roll teen queen, the dungaree doll, the angel baby who just has to make a scene."[&#8202;](#ref-back-324-0)[\[324\]](#refnote-324) In a review of _...Baby One More Time_, Stephen Thomas Erlewine of AllMusic described her music as a "blend of infectious, rap-inflected dance-pop and smooth balladry".[&#8202;](#ref-back-325-0)[\[325\]](#refnote-325) _Oops!... I Did It Again_ saw Spears working with several [R&B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_R&B "Contemporary R&B") producers to create "a combination of bubblegum, urban soul, and [raga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raga "Raga")".[&#8202;](#ref-back-326-0)[\[326\]](#refnote-326) Her third studio album, _Britney_, derived from the teen pop niche "\[&#8202;](#ref-back-britney-1)[r\]hythmically and melodically", but was described as "sharper, tougher than what came before", incorporating genres such as R&B, disco, and funk.[\[52\]](#refnote-britney)[&#8202;](#ref-back-NMEbritney-0)[\[327\]](#refnote-NMEbritney)

Spears has explored and heavily incorporated the genres of [electropop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electropop "Electropop")[&#8202;](#ref-back-328-0)[\[328\]](#refnote-328)[&#8202;](#ref-back-ladailynews-0)[\[329\]](#refnote-ladailynews) and [dance music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dance_music "Dance music") in her records, as well as influences of [urban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_contemporary "Urban contemporary") and [hip-hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hip-hop "Hip-hop"), which are most present on _In the Zone_ and _Blackout_. _In the Zone_ also experiments with [Euro trance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trance_music "Trance music"), [reggae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reggae "Reggae"), and [Middle Eastern music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Eastern_music "Middle Eastern music").[&#8202;](#ref-back-ladailynews-1)[\[329\]](#refnote-ladailynews)[&#8202;](#ref-back-allmusicitz-0)[\[330\]](#refnote-allmusicitz)[&#8202;](#ref-back-allmusicblackout-0)[\[331\]](#refnote-allmusicblackout) _Femme Fatale_ and _Britney Jean_ were also heavily influenced by [electronic music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_music "Electronic music") genres.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Allmusicff-0)[\[332\]](#refnote-Allmusicff)[&#8202;](#ref-back-guardianbj-0)[\[333\]](#refnote-guardianbj) Spears's ninth studio album, _Glory_, is more eclectic and experimental than her previously released work. She commented that it "took a lot of time... it's really different... there are like two or three songs that go in the direction of more urban that I've wanted to do for a long time now, and I just haven't really done that."[&#8202;](#ref-back-334-0)[\[334\]](#refnote-334)

_...Baby One More Time_ and _Oops!... I Did It Again_ address themes such as love and relationships from a teenager's point of view.[&#8202;](#ref-back-ewney2001-0)[\[335\]](#refnote-ewney2001)[&#8202;](#ref-back-336-0)[\[336\]](#refnote-336) Following the massive commercial success of her first two studio albums, Spears's team and producers wanted to maintain the formula that took her to the top of the charts.[&#8202;](#ref-back-ewney2001-1)[\[335\]](#refnote-ewney2001) Spears, however, was no longer satisfied with the sound and themes covered on her records. She co-wrote five songs and chose each track's producer on her third studio album, _Britney_, whose lyrics address the subjects of reaching adulthood, sexuality, and self-discovery.[&#8202;](#ref-back-britney-2)[\[52\]](#refnote-britney)[&#8202;](#ref-back-ewney2001-2)[\[335\]](#refnote-ewney2001)[&#8202;](#ref-back-NMEbritney-1)[\[327\]](#refnote-NMEbritney) Sex, dancing, freedom, and love continued to be Spears's main subjects on her subsequent albums.[&#8202;](#ref-back-allmusicitz-1)[\[330\]](#refnote-allmusicitz)[&#8202;](#ref-back-allmusicblackout-1)[\[331\]](#refnote-allmusicblackout)[&#8202;](#ref-back-guardianbj-1)[\[333\]](#refnote-guardianbj)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Allmusicff-1)[\[332\]](#refnote-Allmusicff) Her fifth studio effort, _Blackout_, also addresses issues such as fame and media scrutiny, including on the song "Piece of Me".[&#8202;](#ref-back-allmusicblackout-2)[\[331\]](#refnote-allmusicblackout)[&#8202;](#ref-back-guardian-0)[\[337\]](#refnote-guardian)

Spears's music has also been noted for some catchphrases. The opening in her debut single "...Baby One More Time", "Oh, baby baby" is considered to be one of her signature lines and has been parodied in the media by various artists such as [Nicole Scherzinger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicole_Scherzinger "Nicole Scherzinger") and Ariana Grande.[&#8202;](#ref-back-338-0)[\[338\]](#refnote-338) It has been used in various forms throughout her music, such as simply, "baby" and "oh baby", as well as the _Blackout_ track, "Ooh Ooh Baby". On the initial development of "...Baby One More Time", [Barry Weiss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Weiss "Barry Weiss") noted Spears's inception of the catchphrase from her strange [ad-libbing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ad-libbing "Ad-libbing") during the recording of the song. He commented further, "We thought it was really weird at first. It was strange. It was not the way [Max](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Martin "Max Martin") wrote it. But it worked! We thought it could be a really good opening salvo for her."[&#8202;](#ref-back-339-0)[\[339\]](#refnote-339) The opening line in "Gimme More", "It's Britney, bitch" has become another signature phrase.[&#8202;](#ref-back-bustle17-0)[\[340\]](#refnote-bustle17) An early review of _Blackout_ suggested the phrase was "simply laughable".[&#8202;](#ref-back-bustle17-1)[\[340\]](#refnote-bustle17) Amy Roberts of _[Bustle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bustle_(magazine) "Bustle (magazine)")_ called it "an indelible cultural turning point, transforming a frenetic, floundering moment in the superstar's career to one of strength and empowerment".[&#8202;](#ref-back-bustle17-2)[\[340\]](#refnote-bustle17)

### Voice

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Gnome-mime-audio-openclipart.svg/60px-Gnome-mime-audio-openclipart.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)

["You Got It All"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Britney_Spears_-_You_Got_It_All.ogg "File:Britney Spears - You Got It All.ogg")

[Play audio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Britney_Spears_-_You_Got_It_All.ogg "Play audio") Duration: 29 seconds.0:29

The cover of [the Jets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jets_(Minnesota_band) "The Jets (Minnesota band)")'s song was Spears's first recording for her then-label Jive in 1997.[&#8202;](#ref-back-341-0)[\[341\]](#refnote-341) The prominent use of [melisma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melisma "Melisma") and [vibrato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vibrato "Vibrato") as she continuously raises key into a high range was viewed as a [Mariah Carey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariah_Carey "Mariah Carey") influence.[&#8202;](#ref-back-342-0)[\[342\]](#refnote-342)

* * *

["Freakshow"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Britney_Spears_-_Freakshow.ogg "File:Britney Spears - Freakshow.ogg")

[Play audio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Britney_Spears_-_Freakshow.ogg "Play audio") Duration: 21 seconds.0:21

The track is built around the 'wobbler' effect of [dubstep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubstep "Dubstep"), and her vocals are pitched down low, making her sound masculine.[&#8202;](#ref-back-guardian-1)[\[337\]](#refnote-guardian)[&#8202;](#ref-back-343-0)[\[343\]](#refnote-343) She has been credited as one of the artists who prompted the genre within a pop mainstream audience.[&#8202;](#ref-back-344-0)[\[344\]](#refnote-344)

* * *

_Problems playing these files? See [media help](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Media "Help:Media")._

Spears is a [soprano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soprano "Soprano").[&#8202;](#ref-back-345-0)[\[345\]](#refnote-345)[&#8202;](#ref-back-SopranoToBass-0)[\[346\]](#refnote-SopranoToBass)[&#8202;](#ref-back-billboardvmas-0)[\[347\]](#refnote-billboardvmas)[&#8202;](#ref-back-350-0)[\[nb 1\]](#refnote-350) Before her breakthrough success, she is described as having sung "much deeper than her highly recognizable trademark voice of today", with Eric Foster White, who worked with Spears on her debut album, _[...Baby One More Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/...Baby_One_More_Time_(album) "...Baby One More Time (album)")_, was said to have shaped her vocals "over the course of a month" upon being signed to [Jive Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jive_Records "Jive Records") "to where it is today—distinctively, unmistakably Britney".[&#8202;](#ref-back-hughes145-2)[\[20\]](#refnote-hughes145) Rami Yacoub, who co-produced Spears's debut album with lyricist Max Martin, commented, "I know from [Denniz Pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denniz_Pop "Denniz Pop") and Max's previous productions, when we do songs, there's kind of a nasal thing. With N' Sync and the Backstreet Boys, we had to push for that mid-nasal voice. When Britney did that, she got this kind of raspy, sexy voice."[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTEBronson2003377-0)[\[350\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTEBronson2003377)

Guy Blackman of _[The Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Age "The Age")_ wrote that "\[&#8202;](#ref-back-The_Age-0)[t\]he thing about Spears, though, is that her biggest songs, no matter how committee-created or impossibly polished, have always been convincing because of her delivery, her commitment and her presence.... Spears expresses perfectly the conflicting urges of adolescence, the tension between chastity and sexual experience, between hedonism and responsibility, between confidence and vulnerability."[\[351\]](#refnote-The_Age) Producer [William Orbit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Orbit "William Orbit"), who worked with Spears on her album _[Britney Jean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britney_Jean "Britney Jean")_, stated regarding her vocals: "\[&#8202;](#ref-back-353-0)[Britney\] didn't get so big just because \[she\] put on great shows; \[she\] got to be that way because \[her voice is\] unique: you hear two words and you know who is singing."[\[352\]](#refnote-353)

Spears has also been criticized for her reliance on [Auto-Tune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auto-Tune "Auto-Tune")[&#8202;](#ref-back-354-0)[\[353\]](#refnote-354) and her vocals being "over-processed" on records.[&#8202;](#ref-back-355-0)[\[354\]](#refnote-355) Erlewine criticized Spears's singing abilities in a review of her _Blackout_ album, stating: "Never the greatest vocalist, her thin squawk could be dismissed early in her career as an adolescent learning the ropes, but nearly a decade later her singing hasn't gotten any better, even if the studio tools to masquerade her weaknesses have."[&#8202;](#ref-back-allmusicblackout-3)[\[331\]](#refnote-allmusicblackout) Joan Anderman of _[The Boston Globe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Boston_Globe "The Boston Globe")_ remarked that "Spears sounds robotic, nearly inhuman, on her records, so processed is her voice by digital pitch-shifters and synthesizers."[&#8202;](#ref-back-356-0)[\[355\]](#refnote-356)

Kayla Upadhyaya of _[The Michigan Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Michigan_Daily "The Michigan Daily")_ has provided a different point of view, stating: "Auto-tuned and over-processed vocals define \[&#8202;](#ref-back-357-0)[Spears\]'s voice as an artist, and in her music, auto-tune isn't so much a gimmick as it is an instrument used to highlight, contort and make a statement."[\[356\]](#refnote-357) Adam Markovitz of _[Entertainment Weekly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entertainment_Weekly "Entertainment Weekly")_ opines that "Spears is no technical singer, that's for sure. But backed by Martin and [Dr. Luke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr._Luke "Dr. Luke")'s wall of sound, her vocals melt into a mix of babytalk coo and coital panting that is, in its own overprocessed way, just as iconic and propulsive as [Michael Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson "Michael Jackson")'s yips or [Eminem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eminem "Eminem")'s snarls."[&#8202;](#ref-back-ew2011-0)[\[357\]](#refnote-ew2011)

### Stage performances and videos

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/IUSA_Circus_Tour.jpg/250px-IUSA_Circus_Tour.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:IUSA_Circus_Tour.jpg) Spears performing on [The Circus Starring Britney Spears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Circus_Starring_Britney_Spears "The Circus Starring Britney Spears") tour in 2009

Spears is known for her stage performances, particularly the elaborate dance routines which incorporate "belly-dancing and tempered erotic moves" that are credited with influencing "dance-heavy acts" such as [Danity Kane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danity_Kane "Danity Kane") and [the Pussycat Dolls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pussycat_Dolls "The Pussycat Dolls").[&#8202;](#ref-back-abad1-0)[\[358\]](#refnote-abad1)[&#8202;](#ref-back-vogueus-0)[\[359\]](#refnote-vogueus) In 2011, _Rolling Stone_ readers voted Spears their second-favorite dancing musician, after Michael Jackson.[&#8202;](#ref-back-361-0)[\[360\]](#refnote-361) Spears is described as being much more shy than her stage persona suggests.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Source1-1)[\[87\]](#refnote-Source1)[&#8202;](#ref-back-vicemb-0)[\[361\]](#refnote-vicemb)[&#8202;](#ref-back-363-0)[\[362\]](#refnote-363) She said that performing is "a boost to \[&#8202;](#ref-back-364-0)[her\] confidence. It's like an alter-ego type thing. Something clicks and I go and turn into this different person. I think it's kind of a gift to be able to do that."[\[363\]](#refnote-364) Her 2000, 2001, and 2003 MTV Video Music Awards performances were lauded,[&#8202;](#ref-back-timevmas-0)[\[364\]](#refnote-timevmas) while her 2007 presentation was widely panned by critics, as she "teetered through her dance steps and mouthed only occasional words".[&#8202;](#ref-back-366-0)[\[365\]](#refnote-366) _Billboard_ called her 2016 "comeback" performance at the show "an effective, but not entirely glorious, bid to regain pop superstardom".[&#8202;](#ref-back-billboardvmas-1)[\[347\]](#refnote-billboardvmas)

After her knee injuries and personal problems, Spears's "showmanship" and dance abilities came under criticism.[&#8202;](#ref-back-abad1-1)[\[358\]](#refnote-abad1)[&#8202;](#ref-back-lasvegas2017-0)[\[366\]](#refnote-lasvegas2017) Serge F. Kovaleski of _The New York Times_ watched her [Las Vegas concert residency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britney:_Piece_of_Me "Britney: Piece of Me") in 2016 and stated: "Once a fluid, natural dancer, Ms. Spears can appear stiff, even robotic, today, relying on flailing arms and flashy sets."[&#8202;](#ref-back-368-0)[\[367\]](#refnote-368) _[Las Vegas Sun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Vegas_Sun "Las Vegas Sun")_'s [Robin Leach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robin_Leach "Robin Leach") seemed more impressed over Spears's efforts, saying that she delivered a "flawless performance" on the residency's opening night.[&#8202;](#ref-back-leach-0)[\[368\]](#refnote-leach)

It has been widely reported that Spears lip-syncs during live performances,[&#8202;](#ref-back-vogueus-1)[\[359\]](#refnote-vogueus)[&#8202;](#ref-back-nmelevine-0)[\[369\]](#refnote-nmelevine)[&#8202;](#ref-back-371-0)[\[370\]](#refnote-371) which often prompts criticism from music critics and concert goers.[&#8202;](#ref-back-lasvegas2017-1)[\[366\]](#refnote-lasvegas2017)[&#8202;](#ref-back-372-0)[\[371\]](#refnote-372)[&#8202;](#ref-back-373-0)[\[372\]](#refnote-373) Some, however, claimed that, although she "got plenty of digital support", she "doesn't merely lip-sync" during her live shows.[&#8202;](#ref-back-spin-0)[\[373\]](#refnote-spin) In 2016, Sabrina Weiss of [Refinery29](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refinery29 "Refinery29") referred to her lip-syncing as a "well-known fact that's not even taboo anymore."[&#8202;](#ref-back-375-0)[\[374\]](#refnote-375) Noting on the prevalence of lip-syncing, the _[Los Angeles Daily News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Daily_News "Los Angeles Daily News")_ opined: "In the context of a Britney Spears concert, does it really matter?... you \[&#8202;](#ref-back-376-0)[just\] go for the somewhat-ridiculous spectacle of it all".[\[375\]](#refnote-376) Spears herself has commented on the topic, arguing: "Because I'm dancing so much, I do have a little bit of playback, but there's a mixture of my voice and the playback.... It really pisses me off because I'm busting my ass out there and singing at the same time and nobody ever gives me credit for it".[&#8202;](#ref-back-nmelevine-1)[\[369\]](#refnote-nmelevine)

In 2012, VH1 ranked Spears fourth among the 50 Greatest Women of the Video Era,[&#8202;](#ref-back-vh1mv-0)[\[376\]](#refnote-vh1mv) while _Billboard_ ranked her eighth among Greatest Music Video Artists of All Time in 2020, explaining: "The storylines, the dancing, the _outfits_. Right from the start, the pop princess established the lengths of her creativity with some of the most memorable videos of the last three decades."[&#8202;](#ref-back-bbmva-0)[\[377\]](#refnote-bbmva) She has been retroactively noted as a pioneer for her early career videography.[&#8202;](#ref-back-voxfreebritney-0)[\[378\]](#refnote-voxfreebritney) She conceptualized the "iconic Catholic schoolgirl and cheerleader motif" in the "...Baby One More Time" video, rejecting the animation video idea. She also made the "Oops!... I Did It Again" video "dance-centric rather than space-centric as her producers suggested". She also used her dancer's intuition to help select the beats for each track.[&#8202;](#ref-back-voxfreebritney-1)[\[378\]](#refnote-voxfreebritney)

## Public image

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Britney_Spears_%288514687036%29.jpg/250px-Britney_Spears_%288514687036%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Britney_Spears_(8514687036).jpg) [Wax figure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wax_figure "Wax figure") of Spears on display at [Madame Tussauds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madame_Tussauds "Madame Tussauds") in London[&#8202;](#ref-back-380-0)[\[379\]](#refnote-380)

Upon launching her music career with _...Baby One More Time_, Spears was labeled a [teen idol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teen_idol "Teen idol"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-381-0)[\[380\]](#refnote-381) and _[Rolling Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone "Rolling Stone")_ described her as "the latest model of a classic product: the unneurotic pop star who performs her duties with [vaudevillian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaudevillian "Vaudevillian") pluck and spokesmodel charm."[&#8202;](#ref-back-stone99-7)[\[12\]](#refnote-stone99) The April 1999 cover of _Rolling Stone_ pictured a 17-year-old Spears reclining on a bed, wearing an exposed bra and [hot pants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_pants "Hot pants") while cradling a [Teletubby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teletubby "Teletubby") in one arm.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Bennett_2021-0)[\[381\]](#refnote-Bennett_2021)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Lambe_2013-0)[\[382\]](#refnote-Lambe_2013)[&#8202;](#ref-back-McAlpine_2017-0)[\[383\]](#refnote-McAlpine_2017)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Rolling_Stone_2011-0)[\[384\]](#refnote-Rolling_Stone_2011) The [American Family Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Family_Association "American Family Association") (AFA) decried the image as "a disturbing mix of childhood innocence and adult sexuality"[&#8202;](#ref-back-Lambe_2013-1)[\[382\]](#refnote-Lambe_2013)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Weiss_2020-0)[\[385\]](#refnote-Weiss_2020) and called on "God-loving Americans to boycott stores selling Britney's albums".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Ortiz_2011-0)[\[386\]](#refnote-Ortiz_2011) Spears addressed the outcry, commenting: "What's the big deal? I have strong morals.... I'd do it again. I thought the pictures were fine. And I was tired of being compared to [Debbie Gibson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debbie_Gibson "Debbie Gibson") and all of this bubblegum pop all the time."[&#8202;](#ref-back-388-0)[\[387\]](#refnote-388) Shortly before this, Spears had announced publicly she would remain abstinent until marriage.[&#8202;](#ref-back-foxnews-12)[\[13\]](#refnote-foxnews)[&#8202;](#ref-back-biographybillboard-0)[\[388\]](#refnote-biographybillboard)

An early criticism of Spears dismissed her as "the product of a Swedish songwriting factory that had no real hand in either her music or her persona". _Vox_ editor Constance Grady wrote this was perpetuated from the fact that Spears debuted in the late 1990s, when music was dominated by [rockism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockism "Rockism"), that prizes "so-called authenticity and grittiness of rock above all else". Spears's "slick, breezy pop was an affront to rockist sensibilities, and claiming that Spears was fake was an easy way to dismiss her." Ron Levy for _Rolling Stone_ noted that "I have to tell you, if the record company could have created more than one Britney Spears, they would have done it, and they tried!"[&#8202;](#ref-back-voxfreebritney-2)[\[378\]](#refnote-voxfreebritney)

_Billboard_ opined that, by the time Spears released her second album, _Oops!... I Did It Again_, "There was a shift occurring in both the music and her public image: She was sharper, sexier and singing about more grown-up fare, setting the stage for 2001's _Britney_, which shed her innocent skin and ushered her into adulthood."[&#8202;](#ref-back-billboard2016-0)[\[389\]](#refnote-billboard2016) _Britney_'s lead single, "I'm a Slave 4 U", and its music video were also credited for distancing her from her previous "wholesome bubblegum star" image.[&#8202;](#ref-back-vh130-0)[\[390\]](#refnote-vh130)[&#8202;](#ref-back-nytimes2001-0)[\[391\]](#refnote-nytimes2001) Stephen Thomas Erlewine of AllMusic remarked, "If 2001's _Britney_ was a transitional album, capturing Spears at the point when she [wasn't a girl and not yet a woman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I'm_Not_a_Girl,_Not_Yet_a_Woman "I'm Not a Girl, Not Yet a Woman"), its 2003 follow-up, _In the Zone_, is where she has finally completed that journey and turned into Britney, the Adult Woman." Erlewine likened Spears to fellow singer Christina Aguilera, explaining that both equated "maturity with transparent sexuality and the pounding sounds of nightclubs".[&#8202;](#ref-back-allmusicitz-2)[\[330\]](#refnote-allmusicitz) Brittany Spanos of _[LA Weekly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LA_Weekly "LA Weekly")_ stated that Spears "set the bar for the 'adulthood' transition teen pop stars often struggle with".[&#8202;](#ref-back-spanos-0)[\[392\]](#refnote-spanos)

Spears's erratic behavior and personal problems during 2006–08 were highly publicized[&#8202;](#ref-back-394-0)[\[393\]](#refnote-394) and affected both her career and public image.[&#8202;](#ref-back-allmusicblackout-4)[\[331\]](#refnote-allmusicblackout)[&#8202;](#ref-back-tragedyrs-0)[\[394\]](#refnote-tragedyrs) Erlewine writes that "each new disaster \[&#8202;](#ref-back-allmusicblackout-5)[was\] stripping away any residual sexiness in her public image".[\[331\]](#refnote-allmusicblackout) In a 2008 article, _Rolling Stone_'s Vanessa Grigoriadis described her much-publicized personal issues as "the most public downfall of any star in history".[&#8202;](#ref-back-tragedyrs-1)[\[394\]](#refnote-tragedyrs) Spears later received favorable media attention; _Billboard_ wrote that her appearance at the 2008 MTV Video Music Awards "was a picture of professionalism and poise" after her "disastrous" [performance the previous year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gimme_More#Live_performances "Gimme More"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-396-0)[\[395\]](#refnote-396) while _[Business Insider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_Insider "Business Insider")_ ran an article on how she had "lost control of her life... and then made an incredible career comeback".[&#8202;](#ref-back-397-0)[\[396\]](#refnote-397) In 2017, Spears said: "I think I had to give myself more breaks through my career and take responsibility for my mental health.... I wrote back then, that I was lost and didn't know what to do with myself. I was trying to please everyone around me because that's who I am deep inside. There are moments where I look back and think: 'What the hell was I thinking?'"[&#8202;](#ref-back-398-0)[\[397\]](#refnote-398)

In September 2002, Spears was placed at number eight on [VH1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VH1 "VH1")'s 100 Sexiest Artists list.[&#8202;](#ref-back-399-0)[\[398\]](#refnote-399) She was placed at number one on _[FHM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FHM "FHM")_'s [100 Sexiest Women in the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FHM's_100_Sexiest_Women_(UK) "FHM's 100 Sexiest Women (UK)") list in 2004,[&#8202;](#ref-back-400-0)[\[399\]](#refnote-400) and, in December 2012, _[Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complex_(magazine) "Complex (magazine)")_ ranked her 12th on its 100 Hottest Female Singers of All Time list.[&#8202;](#ref-back-401-0)[\[400\]](#refnote-401) Remarking upon her perceived image as a sex symbol, Spears stated: "When I'm on stage, that's my time to do my thing and go there and be that — and it's fun. It's exhilarating just to be something that you're not. And people tend to believe it."[&#8202;](#ref-back-ewney2001-3)[\[335\]](#refnote-ewney2001) In 2003, _[People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_(magazine) "People (magazine)")_ cited her as one of the 50 Most Beautiful People.[&#8202;](#ref-back-402-0)[\[401\]](#refnote-402)

In interviews, Spears has described aspects of her personality that contrast with her public image. Reflecting on her youth, she said she was "a pretty normal girl, a tomboy."[&#8202;](#ref-back-403-0)[\[402\]](#refnote-403) She has also stated that she is "really just a tomboy at heart," noting her preference for casual clothing such as jeans and sweatpants.[&#8202;](#ref-back-404-0)[\[403\]](#refnote-404) In reference to her personal relationships, she has said, "I've always been a tomboy, I've always ended up being the man in the relationship."[&#8202;](#ref-back-405-0)[\[404\]](#refnote-405)

Spears is recognized as a [gay icon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gay_icon "Gay icon") and received the 2018 [GLAAD Vanguard Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GLAAD_Vanguard_Award "GLAAD Vanguard Award") at the [GLAAD Media Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GLAAD_Media_Awards "GLAAD Media Awards") for her role in "accelerating acceptance for the LGBTQ community".[&#8202;](#ref-back-spearsglaad-1)[\[239\]](#refnote-spearsglaad) She addressed the "unwavering loyalty" and "lack of judgment" of her LGBTQ fans in _Billboard_'s Love Letters to the LGBTQ Community. She said: "Your stories are what inspire me, bring me joy, and make me and my sons strive to be better people."[&#8202;](#ref-back-406-0)[\[405\]](#refnote-406) Manuel Betancourt of _[Vice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_(magazine) "Vice (magazine)")_ wrote about the "queer adoration", especially of gay men, for Spears, and said that "Where other gay icons exude self-possession, Spears's fragile resilience has made her an even more fascinating role model, closer to [Judy Garland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judy_Garland "Judy Garland") than to Lady Gaga... she's a glittering mirror ball, a fractured reflection of those men on the dance floor back onto themselves."[&#8202;](#ref-back-vicemb-1)[\[361\]](#refnote-vicemb) _[HuffPost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HuffPost "HuffPost")_'s Ben Appel attributed Spears's status as a gay icon to her "oh-so-innocent/not that innocent" [Monroe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marilyn_Monroe "Marilyn Monroe")-like sensuality, her sweet, almost saccharine nature, her beyond basic but addictive pop songs, her dance moves, her phoenix-out-of-the-fire comeback from a series of mental health crises, and her unmistakable tenderness. "Britney is [camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_(style) "Camp (style)"). She is a fashion plate. A doll. Britney is a [drag queen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drag_queen "Drag queen")."[&#8202;](#ref-back-407-0)[\[406\]](#refnote-407)

Since her first public appearances, Spears has been a [tabloid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tabloid_journalism "Tabloid journalism") fixture and a [paparazzi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paparazzi "Paparazzi") target.[&#8202;](#ref-back-billboard2016-1)[\[389\]](#refnote-billboard2016)[&#8202;](#ref-back-biographybillboard-1)[\[388\]](#refnote-biographybillboard)[&#8202;](#ref-back-riseandfall-0)[\[407\]](#refnote-riseandfall) According to _[Vanity Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanity_Fair_(magazine) "Vanity Fair (magazine)")_, photos of Spears sold for a combined value of $100 million in 2007.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Lasky_p55-0)[\[408\]](#refnote-Lasky_p55) Steve Huey of AllMusic remarked that "among female singers of \[Spears's\] era... her celebrity star power was rivaled only by [Jennifer Lopez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Lopez "Jennifer Lopez")."[&#8202;](#ref-back-biographybillboard-2)[\[388\]](#refnote-biographybillboard) "Britney Spears" was [Yahoo!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahoo! "Yahoo!")'s most popular search term between 2005 and 2008, and has been in a total of seven different years.[&#8202;](#ref-back-410-0)[\[409\]](#refnote-410) Spears was named as Most Searched Person in the _[Guinness World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_World_Records "Guinness World Records")_ book edition 2007 and 2009.[&#8202;](#ref-back-411-0)[\[410\]](#refnote-411) She was later named as the most searched person of the decade 2000–2009.[&#8202;](#ref-back-412-0)[\[411\]](#refnote-412)

As a public figure, Spears "has never been known to her fans as a politically active, committed—or even aware—entertainer."[&#8202;](#ref-back-wtimes2012-1)[\[322\]](#refnote-wtimes2012) In a 2003 interview with [Tucker Carlson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tucker_Carlson "Tucker Carlson"), she commented on President [George W. Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_W._Bush "George W. Bush") and the [Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_War "Iraq War"), saying that "we should just trust our president in every decision that he makes... and be faithful in what happens".[&#8202;](#ref-back-413-0)[\[412\]](#refnote-413) [Michael Moore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Moore "Michael Moore") included the footage of Spears's answer in his "anti-Bush" documentary _[Fahrenheit 9/11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fahrenheit_9/11 "Fahrenheit 9/11")_, which, according to _[The Washington Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Washington_Times "The Washington Times")_'s James Frazier, presented her "as an example of a naive American blindly trusting a dishonest commander in chief" and fueled the "urban legend" of a "conservative" Spears. Frazier also said that "the few positions she has taken can hardly be considered conservative", such as supporting [same-sex marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage "Same-sex marriage").[&#8202;](#ref-back-wtimes2012-2)[\[322\]](#refnote-wtimes2012) In 2016, Spears posted pictures of a meeting with [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton "Hillary Clinton") on social media. She described Clinton as "an inspiration and \[&#8202;](#ref-back-414-0)[a\] beautiful voice for women around the world".[\[413\]](#refnote-414)

In December 2017, Spears publicly supported the [DREAM Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DREAM_Act "DREAM Act") in the wake of the announcement that [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump") would end the DACA policy, which previously granted undocumented immigrants who came to the country as minors a renewable two-year period of deferred action from deportation. She posted a photo of herself on social media wearing a black T-shirt that reads "We Are All Dreamers" in white letters. The caption read, "Tell Congress to pass the #DreamAct".[&#8202;](#ref-back-415-0)[\[414\]](#refnote-415)

On September 15, 2021, Spears was named one of the [100 most influential people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100 "Time 100") of the year by _[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_(magazine) "Time (magazine)")._ A few days before the editors' list was released, Spears was put at the top of the readers' voting list of which personalities should be included on the annual _Time_ 100 list. Deemed an icon of that year, editors highlighted the impact of her fight against her conservatorship as well as of the #FreeBritney movement.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:2-0)[\[415\]](#refnote-:2)[&#8202;](#ref-back-417-0)[\[416\]](#refnote-417) In October 2021, Spears thanked her fans and the #FreeBritney movement for "freeing me from my conservatorship".[&#8202;](#ref-back-418-0)[\[417\]](#refnote-418) Spears was also named most famous celebrity from Louisiana, in a 2024 study of internet searches by LouisianaBets.com.[&#8202;](#ref-back-419-0)[\[418\]](#refnote-419)

## Legacy

Main article: [Cultural impact of Britney Spears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_impact_of_Britney_Spears "Cultural impact of Britney Spears")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/Britney_Spears-FFT-Toronto_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-Britney_Spears-FFT-Toronto_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Britney_Spears-FFT-Toronto_(cropped).jpg) Spears in 2011

Referred to as the " [Princess of Pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princess_of_Pop "Princess of Pop")",[&#8202;](#ref-back-vibemag-0)[\[419\]](#refnote-vibemag)[&#8202;](#ref-back-rsmillenial-0)[\[420\]](#refnote-rsmillenial) Spears was credited as one of the "driving force\[&#8202;](#ref-back-biographybillboard-3)[s\] behind the return of teen pop in the late 1990s".[\[388\]](#refnote-biographybillboard)[&#8202;](#ref-back-rsspears-1)[\[323\]](#refnote-rsspears) _Rolling Stone_'s Stacy Lambe explained that she "help\[ed\] to usher in a new era for the genre that had gone dormant in the decade that followed [New Kids on the Block](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Kids_on_the_Block "New Kids on the Block").... Spears would lead an army of pop stars... built on slick Max Martin productions, plenty of sexual innuendo and dance-heavy performances. \[&#8202;](#ref-back-rsmillenial-1)[She became\] one of the most successful artists of all time—and a cautionary tale for a generation, whether they paid attention or not."[\[420\]](#refnote-rsmillenial) In a 2021 article for _Time_, [Maura Johnston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maura_Johnston "Maura Johnston") opined that "Spears's legacy as a pop artist is complex, made up of dazzling musical heights and music-business-borne lows". Johnston also commented: "While Spears's catalog is part of the canon that defines the first 20 years of this millennium, one hopes that her public struggles, and the strength she's shown while enduring them, will lead to her cementing her true legacy: Reshaping the machine that turns those songs into cultural touchstones."[&#8202;](#ref-back-422-0)[\[421\]](#refnote-422)

_[Glamour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glamour_(magazine) "Glamour (magazine)")_ contributor Christopher Rosa described her as "one of pop music's defining voices.... When she emerged onto the scene in 1998 with _...Baby One More Time_, the world hadn't seen a performer like her. Not since Madonna had a female artist affected the genre so profoundly."[&#8202;](#ref-back-423-0)[\[422\]](#refnote-423) _Billboard_'s Robert Kelly observed that Spears's "sexy and coy" vocals on her debut single "...Baby One More Time" "kicked off a new era of pop vocal stylings that would influence countless artists to come".[&#8202;](#ref-back-424-0)[\[423\]](#refnote-424) In 2020, _Rolling Stone_ ranked the song at number one on a list of the 100 Greatest Debut Singles of All Time and [Rob Sheffield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rob_Sheffield "Rob Sheffield") described it as "One of those pop manifestos that announces a new sound, a new era, a new century. But most of all, a new star... With "...Baby One More Time", \[&#8202;](#ref-back-425-0)[Spears\] changed the sound of pop forever: It's Britney, bitch. Nothing was ever the same."[\[424\]](#refnote-425)

Spears was at the forefront of the female teen pop explosion starting in 1999 and extending through the 2000s, leading the pack of [Christina Aguilera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christina_Aguilera "Christina Aguilera"), [Jessica Simpson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jessica_Simpson "Jessica Simpson"), and [Mandy Moore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandy_Moore "Mandy Moore").[&#8202;](#ref-back-426-0)[\[425\]](#refnote-426) All of these performers had been developing material in 1998, but the market changed dramatically in December 1998 when Spears's single and video were charting highly. [RCA Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RCA_Records "RCA Records") quickly signed Aguilera and released her debut single to capitalize on Spears's success, producing her debut hit single " [Genie in a Bottle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genie_in_a_Bottle "Genie in a Bottle")".[&#8202;](#ref-back-427-0)[\[426\]](#refnote-427) Simpson consciously modeled her persona as more mature than Spears; her " [I Wanna Love You Forever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Wanna_Love_You_Forever "I Wanna Love You Forever")" charted in September 1999, and her album _[Sweet Kisses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweet_Kisses "Sweet Kisses")_ followed shortly after.[&#8202;](#ref-back-428-0)[\[427\]](#refnote-428)[&#8202;](#ref-back-429-0)[\[428\]](#refnote-429) Moore's first single, " [Candy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candy_(Mandy_Moore_song) "Candy (Mandy Moore song)")", hit the airwaves a month before Simpson's single.[&#8202;](#ref-back-430-0)[\[429\]](#refnote-430) Fueling media stories about their competition for first place, Spears and Aguilera traded barbs but also compliments through the 2000s.[&#8202;](#ref-back-431-0)[\[430\]](#refnote-431)

Alim Kheraj of _[Dazed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dazed "Dazed")_ called Spears "one of pop's most important pioneers".[&#8202;](#ref-back-432-0)[\[431\]](#refnote-432) After eighteen years as a performer, _Billboard_ described her as having "earned her title as one of pop's reigning queens. Since her early days as a Mouseketeer, \[&#8202;](#ref-back-billboard2016-2)[Spears\] has pushed the boundaries of 21st century sounds, paving the way for a generation of artists to shamelessly embrace glossy pop and redefine how one can accrue consistent success in the music industry."[\[389\]](#refnote-billboard2016) _[Entertainment Weekly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entertainment_Weekly "Entertainment Weekly")_'s Adam Markovitz described Spears as "an American institution, as deeply sacred and messed up as pro wrestling or the filibuster."[&#8202;](#ref-back-ew2011-1)[\[357\]](#refnote-ew2011) She was ranked at number six on _Billboard_'s 2024 [Greatest Pop Stars of the 21st Century](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard's_Greatest_Pop_Stars_of_the_21st_Century "Billboard's Greatest Pop Stars of the 21st Century") list[&#8202;](#ref-back-BillboardPopStars-0)[\[432\]](#refnote-BillboardPopStars) and number 14 on its 2025 "Top 100 Women Artists of the 21st Century" list.[&#8202;](#ref-back-434-0)[\[433\]](#refnote-434)

Spears has been credited with redefining [Las Vegas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Vegas "Las Vegas") residencies as a retirement place for musicians. Her debut concert residency [Britney: Piece of Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britney:_Piece_of_Me "Britney: Piece of Me") was described as "the natural evolution of [Celine Dion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celine_Dion "Celine Dion")'s powerhourse Vegas residency, a still-charting star of another generation redefining the role of Strip headliner." _[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes "Forbes")_ named Spears the sixth-highest-earning female musician of 2015. She is credited with influencing and paving the way for other artists's residencies such as [Jennifer Lopez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Lopez "Jennifer Lopez")'s [Jennifer Lopez: All I Have](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Lopez:_All_I_Have "Jennifer Lopez: All I Have"), [Bruno Mars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_Mars "Bruno Mars")'s [Bruno Mars at Park MGM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_Mars_at_Park_MGM "Bruno Mars at Park MGM"), and [Backstreet Boys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Backstreet_Boys "Backstreet Boys")' [Backstreet Boys: Larger Than Life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Backstreet_Boys:_Larger_Than_Life "Backstreet Boys: Larger Than Life").[&#8202;](#ref-back-435-0)[\[434\]](#refnote-435) The arrival of Spears "saw the pop promoters finally tap into the younger crowd arriving in town for a good time".[&#8202;](#ref-back-436-0)[\[435\]](#refnote-436)

Spears's much-publicized personal problems and her subsequent career comeback have inspired some artists. [Gwyneth Paltrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwyneth_Paltrow "Gwyneth Paltrow")'s character in the 2010 film _[Country Strong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_Strong "Country Strong")_ was inspired by Spears's treatment by the media. According to film director Shana Fest, "that's where this movie came from. I mean, I was seeing what was happening in the media to Britney Spears. I think it's tragic how we treat people who give us so much, and we love to see them knocked down to build them back up again, to knock them down again."[&#8202;](#ref-back-437-0)[\[436\]](#refnote-437) [Nicki Minaj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicki_Minaj "Nicki Minaj") has cited Spears's comeback after her much-publicized personal issues as an inspiration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-438-0)[\[437\]](#refnote-438) Spears's hounding by paparazzi and personal problems also inspired [Barry Manilow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Manilow "Barry Manilow")'s album _[15 Minutes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/15_Minutes_(Barry_Manilow_album) "15 Minutes (Barry Manilow album)")_. Manilow said: "She couldn't have a life without them pulling up next to her car and following her and driving her crazy to the point where, that was around the time she shaved off her hair.... We all looked at it in horror... So it seemed like a thing to be writing an album about."[&#8202;](#ref-back-439-0)[\[438\]](#refnote-439) [Bebo Norman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bebo_Norman "Bebo Norman") wrote a song about Spears, called " [Britney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britney_(Bebo_Norman_song) "Britney (Bebo Norman song)")", which was inspired by "culture's make-or-break treatment of celebrities".[&#8202;](#ref-back-440-0)[\[439\]](#refnote-440) Along with [Alicia Silverstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alicia_Silverstone "Alicia Silverstone"), [Christy Turlington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christy_Turlington "Christy Turlington"), and [Naomi Campbell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naomi_Campbell "Naomi Campbell"), Spears has been credited with introducing the [navel piercing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navel_piercing "Navel piercing") to mainstream culture.[&#8202;](#ref-back-441-0)[\[440\]](#refnote-441)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kim_2024_c296-0)[\[441\]](#refnote-Kim_2024_c296)[&#8202;](#ref-back-z542-0)[\[442\]](#refnote-z542)

## Achievements

See also: [List of awards and nominations received by Britney Spears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_Britney_Spears "List of awards and nominations received by Britney Spears")

Spears's awards and accolades include a [Grammy Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy_Award "Grammy Award"); 15 [Guinness World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_World_Records "Guinness World Records"); six [MTV Video Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Awards "MTV Video Music Awards"), including the [Michael Jackson Video Vanguard Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson_Video_Vanguard_Award "Michael Jackson Video Vanguard Award"); seven [_Billboard_ Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard_Music_Awards "Billboard Music Awards"), including the [Millennium Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard_Millennium_Award "Billboard Millennium Award");[&#8202;](#ref-back-444-0)[\[443\]](#refnote-444) the inaugural Radio Disney Icon Award; the [GLAAD Media Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GLAAD_Media_Award "GLAAD Media Award")'s [Vanguard Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GLAAD_Vanguard_Award "GLAAD Vanguard Award"); and a star on the [Hollywood Walk of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame "Hollywood Walk of Fame").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:1-0)[\[444\]](#refnote-:1)

Spears is listed by the _[Guinness World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_World_Records "Guinness World Records")_ as having the "Best-selling album by a teenage solo artist" for her debut studio album, _...Baby One More Time_, which sold over 13 million copies in the United States.[&#8202;](#ref-back-446-0)[\[445\]](#refnote-446) Melissa Ruggieri of the _[Richmond Times-Dispatch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond_Times-Dispatch "Richmond Times-Dispatch")_ reported: "She's also marked for being the best-selling teenage artist. Before she turned 20 in 2001, Spears had sold over 37 million albums worldwide".[&#8202;](#ref-back-447-0)[\[446\]](#refnote-447)

Spears has sold over 150 million records worldwide, making her one of the [best-selling music artists of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_best-selling_music_artists "List of best-selling music artists").[&#8202;](#ref-back-448-0)[\[447\]](#refnote-448)[&#8202;](#ref-back-449-0)[\[448\]](#refnote-449) She also sold more than 70 million records in United States, including 36.9 million digital singles and 33.6 million digital albums.[&#8202;](#ref-back-450-0)[\[449\]](#refnote-450) Spears is further recognized as the best-selling female albums artist of the 2000s in the United States, as well as the fifth overall.[&#8202;](#ref-back-21sells-0)[\[450\]](#refnote-21sells) In December 2009, _Billboard_ ranked Spears the 8th Artist of the 2000s decade in the United States.[&#8202;](#ref-back-bbad-0)[\[451\]](#refnote-bbad) She is one of the few artists in history to have had a number-one single and a number-one studio album in the US during each of the three decades of her career.[&#8202;](#ref-back-453-0)[\[452\]](#refnote-453) With " [3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3_(Britney_Spears_song) "3 (Britney Spears song)")" in 2009 and " [Hold It Against Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hold_It_Against_Me "Hold It Against Me")" in 2011, she became the second artist after Mariah Carey in the Hot 100's history to debut at number one with two or more songs.[&#8202;](#ref-back-hiamrecord-1)[\[148\]](#refnote-hiamrecord) In 2016, Spears ranked at number twenty on _Billboard_'s Greatest Of All Time Top [Dance Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dance_Club_Songs "Dance Club Songs") Artists list.[&#8202;](#ref-back-454-0)[\[453\]](#refnote-454)

## Other ventures

### Product and endorsements

Main articles: [Britney Spears products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britney_Spears_products "Britney Spears products") and [Britney Spears doll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britney_Spears_doll "Britney Spears doll")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Curious.jpg/250px-Curious.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Curious.jpg) Bottle of one of Spears's several perfumes, Curious, released through [Elizabeth Arden, Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Arden,_Inc. "Elizabeth Arden, Inc.")

In 2000, Spears released a limited edition of sunglasses titled Shades of Britney.[&#8202;](#ref-back-455-0)[\[454\]](#refnote-455) In 2001, she signed a deal with shoe company [Skechers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skechers "Skechers"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-456-0)[\[455\]](#refnote-456) and a $7–8 million promotional deal with [Pepsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pepsi "Pepsi"), their biggest entertainment deal at the time.[&#8202;](#ref-back-457-0)[\[456\]](#refnote-457) Aside from numerous commercials with the latter during that year, she also appeared in a 2004 Pepsi television commercial in the theme of "Gladiators" with singers [Beyoncé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beyonc%C3%A9 "Beyoncé"), [Pink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pink_(singer) "Pink (singer)"), and [Enrique Iglesias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrique_Iglesias "Enrique Iglesias").[&#8202;](#ref-back-458-0)[\[457\]](#refnote-458) On June 19, 2002, she released her first multi-platform video game, _[Britney's Dance Beat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britney's_Dance_Beat "Britney's Dance Beat")_, which received positive reviews.[&#8202;](#ref-back-459-0)[\[458\]](#refnote-459)

In March 2009, Spears was announced time as the new face of clothing brand [Candie's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candie's "Candie's").[&#8202;](#ref-back-candy-0)[\[459\]](#refnote-candy) Dari Marder, chief marketing officer for the brand, said: "Everybody loves a comeback and nobody's doing it better than Britney. She's just poised for even greater success."[&#8202;](#ref-back-candy-1)[\[459\]](#refnote-candy) In 2010, Spears designed a limited edition line for the brand, which was released in stores in July 2010.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Vena-1)[\[140\]](#refnote-Vena) In 2011, she teamed up with [Sony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sony "Sony"), [Make Up For Ever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Make_Up_For_Ever "Make Up For Ever"), and [Plenty of Fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plenty_of_Fish "Plenty of Fish") to release her music video for "Hold It Against Me", earning her $500,000 for the product placement.[&#8202;](#ref-back-461-0)[\[460\]](#refnote-461)

Spears teamed up with [Hasbro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasbro "Hasbro") in 2012 to release an exclusive version of _[Twister Dance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twister_(game) "Twister (game)")_, which includes a remix of "Till the World Ends". She was featured on a commercial, which was directed by [Ray Kay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_Kay "Ray Kay"), to promote the game.[&#8202;](#ref-back-462-0)[\[461\]](#refnote-462) Spears was also featured on the commercial of _Twister Dance Rave_, and the game included a Twister remix of " [Circus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circus_(Britney_Spears_song) "Circus (Britney Spears song)")". In March 2018, it was revealed that Spears would be the face of [Kenzo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenzo_(brand) "Kenzo (brand)"), a contemporary French luxury clothing house.[&#8202;](#ref-back-harpersbazaar.com.au-1)[\[237\]](#refnote-harpersbazaar.com.au)

Spears's range of commercial deals and products also includes beauty care products and perfumes. She released her first perfume with [Elizabeth Arden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Arden "Elizabeth Arden"), [Curious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curious_(fragrance) "Curious (fragrance)"), in 2004, which broke the company's first-week gross for a perfume.[&#8202;](#ref-back-foxnews-13)[\[13\]](#refnote-foxnews) By 2009, she had released seven more perfumes, including [Fantasy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fantasy_(fragrance) "Fantasy (fragrance)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-463-0)[\[462\]](#refnote-463)[&#8202;](#ref-back-464-0)[\[463\]](#refnote-464) In 2010, Spears released her eighth fragrance, [Radiance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radiance_(fragrance) "Radiance (fragrance)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-465-0)[\[464\]](#refnote-465)

In 2011, Radiance was reissued as a new perfume titled Cosmic Radiance.[&#8202;](#ref-back-466-0)[\[465\]](#refnote-466) Worldwide, Spears sold over one million bottles in the first five years, with gross receipts of $1.5 billion. In 2016, Spears contacted [Glu Mobile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glu_Mobile "Glu Mobile") to create her own role-playing game, _[Britney Spears: American Dream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britney_Spears:_American_Dream "Britney Spears: American Dream")_. The app officially launched in May 2016 and is compatible with [iOS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IOS "IOS") and [Android](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Android_(operating_system) "Android (operating system)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-billboard.com-1)[\[208\]](#refnote-billboard.com) On June 17, 2016, Spears announced the release of her twentieth fragrance, Private Show.[&#8202;](#ref-back-467-0)[\[466\]](#refnote-467)As of January2018[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Britney_Spears&action=edit), Spears has released 24 fragrances through Elizabeth Arden.[&#8202;](#ref-back-468-0)[\[467\]](#refnote-468)

### Philanthropy

Spears founded The Britney Spears Foundation, a charitable entity set up to help children in need.
