The philosophy behind the Foundation was that music and entertainment has a healing quality that can benefit children.[&#8202;](#ref-back-469-0)[\[468\]](#refnote-469) The Foundation also supported the annual Britney Spears Camp for the Performing Arts, where campers had the opportunity to explore and develop their talents.[&#8202;](#ref-back-470-0)[\[469\]](#refnote-470) In April 2002, through the efforts of Spears and The Britney Spears Foundation, a grant of $1 million was made to the Twin Towers Fund to support the children of uniformed service heroes affected by the [terrorist attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_11_attacks "September 11 attacks") of September 11, 2001, including [New York City Fire Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Fire_Department "New York City Fire Department") and its Emergency Medical Services Command, the [New York City Police Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Police_Department "New York City Police Department"), the [Port Authority of New York and New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Authority_of_New_York_and_New_Jersey_Police_Department "Port Authority of New York and New Jersey Police Department"), the New York State Office of court Administration and other government offices.[&#8202;](#ref-back-471-0)[\[470\]](#refnote-471) However, it was reported in 2008 that the Foundation had a deficit of $200,000.[&#8202;](#ref-back-472-0)[\[471\]](#refnote-472) After Spears went through conservatorship, her father and lawyer Andrew Wallet zeroed out the effort, leading to its closure in 2011.[&#8202;](#ref-back-473-0)[\[472\]](#refnote-473)

On October 30, 2001, Spears, alongside [Bono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bono "Bono") and other popular recording artists under the name "Artists Against AIDS Worldwide", released an album consisting of multiple versions of [Marvin Gaye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marvin_Gaye "Marvin Gaye")'s " [What's Going On](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/What's_Going_On_(Marvin_Gaye_song) "What's Going On (Marvin Gaye song)")", intending to benefit AIDS programs in Africa and other impoverished regions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-474-0)[\[473\]](#refnote-474) In 2005, Spears donated $350,000 to [Music Rising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_Rising "Music Rising") for [Hurricane Katrina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Katrina "Hurricane Katrina") victims.[&#8202;](#ref-back-lts-0)[\[474\]](#refnote-lts) Spears has also helped several charities during her career, including Madonna's Kabbalah-based Spirituality for Kids,[&#8202;](#ref-back-476-0)[\[475\]](#refnote-476) cancer charity Gilda's Club Worldwide,[&#8202;](#ref-back-477-0)[\[476\]](#refnote-477) the Promises Foundation, and the [United Way](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Way "United Way"), with the latter two focused on giving families from various disadvantaged situations new hope and stable foundations for the future.[&#8202;](#ref-back-lts-1)[\[474\]](#refnote-lts)

On October 24, 2015, Spears donated $120,000 to the Nevada Childhood Cancer Foundation (NCCF).[&#8202;](#ref-back-478-0)[\[477\]](#refnote-478) In addition, $1 of each ticket sale from her Las Vegas residency, Britney: Piece of Me, was donated to the foundation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-479-0)[\[478\]](#refnote-479) Spears fundraised for the charity through social media and sold limited edition merchandise, with all proceeds going to the NCCF.[&#8202;](#ref-back-480-0)[\[479\]](#refnote-480) On October 27, 2016, Spears partnered with [Zappos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zappos "Zappos") and XCYCLE to host the _Britney Spears Piece of Me Charity Ride_ in Boca Park, Las Vegas to raise additional money toward her goal of $1 million for the NCCF, with $450,000 having already been raised from her ticket sales and merchandise.[&#8202;](#ref-back-481-0)[\[480\]](#refnote-481) Participants were entered for a chance to win a spin class with Spears herself. The event ultimately went on to raise $553,130.[&#8202;](#ref-back-482-0)[\[481\]](#refnote-482) The fundraising ultimately led to the development of the NCCF Britney Spears Campus in Las Vegas, which saw its grand opening on November 4, 2017.[&#8202;](#ref-back-483-0)[\[482\]](#refnote-483) Spears also regularly participates in [Spirit Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spirit_Day "Spirit Day") to combat [bullying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bullying "Bullying"), in particular bullying of [LGBTQ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ "LGBTQ") youth.[&#8202;](#ref-back-484-0)[\[483\]](#refnote-484)

In March 2020, Spears was participating in the #DoYourPartChallenge, which entails helping people with anything they might need during the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic "COVID-19 pandemic"). She told fans to send her messages on Instagram if they needed supportive words during the coronavirus pandemic, with Spears picking three fans.[&#8202;](#ref-back-485-0)[\[484\]](#refnote-485) In February 2024, Spears partnered with New York City dessert shop Glace, to create the "Britney Brûlée" dessert, with a portion of the proceeds from its sales being donated to [The Trevor Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Trevor_Project "The Trevor Project").[&#8202;](#ref-back-486-0)[\[485\]](#refnote-486)
